File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/specs/admin/spec.md
================
## Purpose

The business operating tool for a one-person operator — dashboard, catalog management, bulk import, content management, data quality, analytics views, and affiliate settings — where product operations never require Sanity Studio.

## ADDED Requirements

### Requirement: Admin is a business operating tool
The system SHALL provide an admin treated as a business operating tool, not just a CMS. It SHALL help the owner operate the affiliate business without needing to understand Sanity, databases, code, or technical maintenance, and SHALL NOT depend on opening Sanity Studio for normal product management. The admin screens SHALL exist as application pages (dashboard, catalog, content, analytics, affiliate, system) with full interactions, backed by real data.

#### Scenario: Product managed without Sanity Studio
- **WHEN** the owner edits a product in the admin
- **THEN** the change is saved to Supabase and no Sanity Studio interaction is required

#### Scenario: Admin covers business tasks
- **WHEN** the owner needs to run the business
- **THEN** the admin provides screens for dashboard, catalog, content, analytics, affiliate, and system tasks

### Requirement: Admin data pages render on the server (owner decision 2026-08-24)
Every admin page that displays catalog or content data SHALL be a React Server Component that fetches its data directly during server rendering and passes it to client components as props. Client components SHALL handle interactivity only (filters, sorting UI, pagination controls, dialogs, action buttons) and SHALL NOT fetch a page's initial list data. Initial reads SHALL NEVER depend on browser-executed JavaScript (no post-render effect firing dynamic-imported server actions for reads). Mutations remain server actions and each mutation SHALL refresh the affected server-rendered data via revalidation, so lists update without a manual browser reload. A failed server-side fetch SHALL render a visible error state with retry; an empty dataset SHALL render its normal empty state.

#### Scenario: Client navigation arrives populated
- **WHEN** the owner navigates via the sidebar to any admin data page
- **THEN** the page arrives with its data already rendered — the browser fires ZERO read requests for the initial list

#### Scenario: Mutation refreshes the visible list
- **WHEN** the owner creates, edits, or deletes an item from an admin data page
- **THEN** the server-rendered list reflects the change without a manual full-page reload

#### Scenario: Server fetch fails loudly
- **WHEN** the database query behind an admin data page fails
- **THEN** the page renders a visible error state with retry instead of an empty success

#### Scenario: Deep link restores a filtered view
- **WHEN** the owner opens a URL carrying filter/pagination parameters for a large list (e.g., products)
- **THEN** the server component renders that exact filtered view directly

### Requirement: Product admin
The system SHALL let the owner search products, filter products, edit products, publish/unpublish products, assign categories, assign brands, add/change ASIN, manage images, edit product information (including sizes and product-distinct details), delete products, and perform bulk actions. The admin SHALL query Supabase directly through the product data layer. The product list SHALL default to showing all statuses (published and unpublished).

- **ASIN is per-product**: the ASIN field belongs to one product only and SHALL be edited per product; bulk actions SHALL NOT include product-distinct fields such as ASIN.
- **Bulk actions** SHALL cover only fields shared across products (status, category, brand, delete).
- **Full editor reachable from the catalog**: the full product editor (images, sizes, description, technical details) SHALL be reachable from the product list in one click; quick-edit dialogs are for fast field tweaks only.

#### Scenario: Search and filter products
- **WHEN** the owner searches or filters the product list
- **THEN** the admin returns matching products from Supabase through the product service

#### Scenario: Publish/unpublish product
- **WHEN** the owner publishes or unpublishes a product
- **THEN** the product status is updated in Supabase and customer-facing queries reflect the change

#### Scenario: Assign category and brand
- **WHEN** the owner assigns a category or brand to a product
- **THEN** the product's classification is updated in the product database

#### Scenario: Add or change ASIN
- **WHEN** the owner adds or changes a product's ASIN
- **THEN** the product record in Supabase is updated, and saving other product fields never erases an existing ASIN

#### Scenario: Unpublished products visible by default
- **WHEN** the owner opens the product list without choosing a status filter
- **THEN** both published and unpublished products are shown

#### Scenario: Full editor reached from the catalog
- **WHEN** the owner clicks a product in the catalog
- **THEN** the full product editor opens (images, sizes, description, technical details) without typing a URL

### Requirement: Model hierarchy management
The system SHALL provide a models management surface in the admin showing the hierarchy as brand → root model → submodel → product model, with product counts on product models. The owner SHALL be able to create root models, create child models under a parent, rename, reparent, and delete models. A model referenced by products or children SHALL NOT be deletable (clear error message). Creating a second variant under a node SHALL trigger the automatic restructure: the node becomes a submodel and each variant becomes a product model (see product-catalog spec — model hierarchy with derived node types).

#### Scenario: Models page shows the tree
- **WHEN** the owner opens the models page
- **THEN** the tree shows root models, submodels, and product models with their single product per product model

#### Scenario: Variant add restructures
- **WHEN** the owner adds a product that would share a node with an existing product
- **THEN** the tree restructures so each product has its own product model, and the page reflects the new tree

#### Scenario: Protected delete
- **WHEN** the owner tries to delete a model that products or child models reference
- **THEN** the delete is blocked with a clear error message

### Requirement: Bulk import workflow
The system SHALL provide a bulk import workflow: upload, read file, validate, show errors, show preview, confirm, save to Supabase, show result. The system SHALL clearly report products that will be created, products that will be updated, duplicate products, missing ASIN, missing images, missing categories, invalid values, and failed rows. The user SHALL never have to guess whether an import worked. The validation/preview data SHALL be computed from the real Supabase catalog, never from mock constants.

**Import formats:** the upload SHALL accept Microsoft Excel workbooks (`.xlsx`) as well as CSV files (owner order 2026-08-24). Both formats flow through the SAME parse → validate → preview → confirm → save pipeline and produce identical validation and result reports; format only changes how raw rows are read, never how they are checked or saved.

#### Scenario: Excel workbook accepted
- **WHEN** the owner uploads an `.xlsx` file to the Import Center
- **THEN** it is parsed and validated exactly like a CSV upload, producing the same preview, error report, and result report

#### Scenario: Import with errors shows them before saving
- **WHEN** an uploaded file contains invalid rows
- **THEN** the system shows the errors and does not save until the owner confirms the preview

#### Scenario: Import result is explicit
- **WHEN** the owner confirms an import
- **THEN** the system saves to Supabase and shows a result report of what was created, updated, or failed — with per-row failure reasons

#### Scenario: Duplicate products reported
- **WHEN** an upload contains products that already exist
- **THEN** the system reports them as duplicates and handles them as updates when confirmed

#### Scenario: Import center shows real numbers
- **WHEN** the owner opens the import center
- **THEN** the validation report is computed from the Supabase catalog and matches direct database counts

### Requirement: Data Quality
The system SHALL provide a Data Quality area that answers "what is wrong with my catalog": counts of products missing ASIN, missing images, missing category, duplicate products, and invalid products. Each problem SHALL be clickable to view the affected products.

#### Scenario: Data Quality counts
- **WHEN** the owner opens Data Quality
- **THEN** the system shows counts such as products missing ASIN, products missing images, products missing category, duplicates, and invalid products

#### Scenario: Problem drills into affected products
- **WHEN** the owner clicks a Data Quality problem
- **THEN** the system shows the list of affected products

### Requirement: Admin dashboard
The system SHALL provide a dashboard as the owner's starting point showing catalog health (total products, active products, products needing attention, missing ASIN, missing images, missing categories), affiliate performance (Amazon clicks, top products, top brands, top categories), recent activity (recent imports, recent product updates, recent content changes), and tasks (products missing ASIN, products missing images, failed imports, other data problems). The dashboard SHALL be actionable.

#### Scenario: Dashboard shows catalog health
- **WHEN** the owner opens the dashboard
- **THEN** it shows total and active products plus products needing attention

#### Scenario: Dashboard shows tasks
- **WHEN** the owner opens the dashboard
- **THEN** it lists actionable tasks such as products missing ASIN, products missing images, and failed imports

### Requirement: Content management in admin
The system SHALL let the owner manage homepage content, navigation, and media through the admin. Homepage content and navigation SHALL be stored as text in Supabase, with navigation pointing at product-database categories. Images SHALL be uploaded to Sanity and their links stored in Supabase.

#### Scenario: Homepage content edited in admin
- **WHEN** the owner edits homepage content in the admin
- **THEN** the content is saved to Supabase and the website reflects the change without touching product data

#### Scenario: Image uploaded in admin
- **WHEN** the owner uploads an image in the admin media area
- **THEN** the image asset is stored in Sanity and its link is saved in Supabase for reference

### Requirement: Affiliate settings and link generator
The system SHALL let the owner manage Amazon settings and generate Amazon links/cart links from the admin.

#### Scenario: Affiliate settings saved
- **WHEN** the owner saves Amazon Associate settings
- **THEN** the affiliate service uses the new configuration for link generation

#### Scenario: Link generator
- **WHEN** the owner uses the link/cart generator
- **THEN** the admin produces the Amazon destination for selected products using the affiliate service

### Requirement: Admin data ownership
Each type of admin data SHALL have one owner: products, bulk import, categories, and brands go to Supabase; homepage content, navigation, and all text content go to Supabase; images go to Sanity with links stored in Supabase; analytics come from the analytics data source.

#### Scenario: Brand created in admin
- **WHEN** the owner creates a brand in the admin
- **THEN** the brand is stored in Supabase

#### Scenario: Category created in admin
- **WHEN** the owner creates a category in the admin
- **THEN** the category is stored in Supabase and product classification references it

### Requirement: Destructive actions require confirmation
Every destructive admin action (delete product, bulk delete, delete brand, delete category, delete model, delete import rows) SHALL require an explicit confirmation before executing — a confirmation dialog stating what will be deleted and how many items are affected. No destructive action SHALL execute on a single click.

#### Scenario: Delete product asks for confirmation
- **WHEN** the owner clicks delete on a product (or on a bulk selection)
- **THEN** the admin shows a confirmation dialog naming the product(s) before any row is deleted

### Requirement: Action buttons show their state
Every admin action button SHALL show distinct UI states: idle, busy (pending), success, and error. While an action runs, the button SHALL be disabled and show progress; on failure, the error SHALL be visible (inline or toast) and the button SHALL return to idle.

#### Scenario: Saving shows progress and error
- **WHEN** the owner submits a save or bulk action
- **THEN** the button shows a busy state while running, a success confirmation when done, and a visible error with idle state restored if it fails

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/specs/amazon-affiliate/spec.md
================
## Purpose

The isolated Amazon affiliate service — the only place that builds Amazon product and cart links, holds affiliate configuration, and validates Amazon product data.

## ADDED Requirements

### Requirement: One place for Amazon affiliate behavior
The system SHALL provide one service responsible for all Amazon affiliate behavior: building a product link, building a cart link, retrieving affiliate configuration, and validating Amazon product data. The rest of the application SHALL NOT build Amazon URLs manually.

#### Scenario: Product link built by service
- **WHEN** any part of the application needs an Amazon product link
- **THEN** it asks the affiliate service and the service builds the link

#### Scenario: No manual Amazon URLs elsewhere
- **WHEN** code outside the affiliate service needs an Amazon destination
- **THEN** it does not construct the Amazon URL itself

### Requirement: Product link generation
The system SHALL generate a link to a product on Amazon from the product's ASIN, quantity, and the configured Amazon Associate/affiliate information.

#### Scenario: Buy on Amazon link
- **WHEN** a customer clicks Buy on Amazon for a product
- **THEN** the service returns a link to that product on Amazon carrying the affiliate information

### Requirement: Cart link generation
The system SHALL attempt to generate an Amazon cart destination link containing multiple selected products from their ASINs and quantities.

#### Scenario: Multi-product Amazon cart link
- **WHEN** a customer has multiple products selected
- **THEN** the service attempts to build an Amazon cart link containing the selected ASINs and quantities

### Requirement: Affiliate configuration
The system SHALL store and expose the Amazon Associate/affiliate configuration in one place through the service.

#### Scenario: Affiliate settings applied to links
- **WHEN** the affiliate service builds a link
- **THEN** the link includes the configured Amazon Associate information

### Requirement: Amazon product data validation
The system SHALL validate Amazon product data — at minimum, presence and well-formedness of the ASIN — and SHALL report products with missing or invalid Amazon data.

#### Scenario: Missing ASIN reported
- **WHEN** a product has no ASIN
- **THEN** validation reports the product as missing Amazon data

### Requirement: Product database stores identifiers only
The product database SHALL store only the Amazon product identifier and related product information; it SHALL NOT contain Amazon URL logic. The frontend SHALL NOT need to understand Amazon's URL rules.

#### Scenario: ASIN stored in product database
- **WHEN** a product record contains Amazon data
- **THEN** it contains the ASIN identifier, and all URL construction happens in the affiliate service

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/specs/analytics/spec.md
================
## Purpose

Business-focused analytics measuring the path from Athletica to Amazon — product views, Amazon clicks, click-through rate, and top products, brands, and categories — so the owner knows what is getting attention.

## ADDED Requirements

### Requirement: Measure the business path to Amazon
The system SHALL measure product views, Amazon clicks, and click-through rate, and SHALL surface top products, top brands, and top categories. Search activity and traffic sources SHALL be captured where available.

#### Scenario: Product view recorded
- **WHEN** a customer views a product
- **THEN** the system records a product view

#### Scenario: Amazon click recorded
- **WHEN** a customer clicks through to Amazon
- **THEN** the system records an Amazon click for that product

#### Scenario: Top products shown
- **WHEN** the owner views analytics
- **THEN** the system shows the top products, brands, and categories by clicks or views

### Requirement: Analytics answer business questions
The system SHALL present analytics that answer: what is getting attention, what are people clicking, which products should the owner care about, and what needs attention. The system SHALL NOT build complicated analytics just for the sake of having charts.

#### Scenario: Dashboard answers what is getting attention
- **WHEN** the owner opens the analytics overview
- **THEN** it shows what products are getting views and clicks

#### Scenario: No speculative analytics
- **WHEN** analytics are designed
- **THEN** only measurements that serve the business goal of getting people from Athletica to Amazon are included

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/specs/cart/spec.md
================
## Purpose

The product selection cart — customers add, remove, and change quantity of products identified by product ID, and the cart hands off to Amazon for purchase through the Amazon Affiliate Service.

## ADDED Requirements

### Requirement: Cart is a product selection cart
The system SHALL provide a cart that is a product selection cart, not a payment cart. Customers SHALL be able to add a product, remove a product, change quantity, and view selected products. The purchase happens on Amazon, not on Athletica.

#### Scenario: Add product to cart
- **WHEN** a customer adds a product to the cart
- **THEN** the product appears in the cart with its quantity

#### Scenario: Remove product from cart
- **WHEN** a customer removes a product from the cart
- **THEN** the product no longer appears in the cart

#### Scenario: Change quantity
- **WHEN** a customer changes the quantity of a product in the cart
- **THEN** the cart reflects the new quantity

### Requirement: Cart stores product identifiers
The system SHALL store enough information to identify the products in the cart — preferring product IDs and quantities rather than copying the entire product object — and SHALL retrieve current product information when the cart is viewed.

#### Scenario: Cart view shows current product info
- **WHEN** a customer views the cart
- **THEN** the application retrieves and displays the current product information for the stored product IDs

### Requirement: Cart hands off to Amazon
The system SHALL send the customer to Amazon using the cart's product ASINs, quantities, and the affiliate's Amazon Associate information. For a single product the system SHALL direct the customer to that product on Amazon; for multiple products the system SHALL attempt to create the appropriate Amazon cart destination containing the selected products. The exact Amazon multi-product URL/request format SHALL be verified against current Amazon affiliate capabilities during implementation.

#### Scenario: Single product to Amazon
- **WHEN** a customer clicks Buy on Amazon for one product in the cart
- **THEN** the system directs the customer to that product on Amazon using its ASIN and the affiliate information

#### Scenario: Multiple products to Amazon
- **WHEN** a customer clicks Continue to Amazon with multiple products in the cart
- **THEN** the system attempts to direct the customer to an Amazon cart destination containing the selected products

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/specs/content/spec.md
================
## Purpose

Website content and media — homepage sections, banners, marketing copy, editorial text, and navigation — stored as text in Supabase, with image assets stored in Sanity and referenced by links from Supabase records.

## ADDED Requirements

### Requirement: All text content lives in Supabase
The system SHALL store all website text content — homepage banners, homepage sections, promotional content, marketing text, editorial content, navigation, and settings — in Supabase. Sanity SHALL NOT store text content and SHALL NOT store complete product records or copies of products.

#### Scenario: Homepage banner edited in Supabase
- **WHEN** the owner edits a homepage banner
- **THEN** the change is saved to Supabase and appears on the website, with no text stored in Sanity

#### Scenario: No product copies in Sanity
- **WHEN** a product is displayed on the homepage
- **THEN** no duplicate product record is created in Sanity

### Requirement: Sanity stores image assets only
The system SHALL use Sanity exclusively to store image assets. Supabase records — products, brands, homepage sections, editorial content — SHALL hold the links needed to display those images. Sanity SHALL NOT become the owner of any product or content record because an image is stored there.

#### Scenario: Product image served from Sanity
- **WHEN** a product page displays a product image
- **THEN** the image asset is served from Sanity using the link stored in the Supabase product record

#### Scenario: Brand logo served from Sanity
- **WHEN** a brand page displays a brand logo
- **THEN** the logo asset is served from Sanity using the link stored in the Supabase brand record

### Requirement: Homepage sections reference products
The system SHALL let homepage sections reference products by query (e.g., category + limit) instead of copying complete product records, and the website SHALL fetch the referenced products from Supabase through the product data layer.

#### Scenario: Featured section resolves to current products
- **WHEN** a homepage section declares category = football-boots, limit = 12
- **THEN** the website fetches up to 12 current products from that category from Supabase

### Requirement: Navigation as content without product classification
The system SHALL manage website navigation (the menu customers see) as content stored in Supabase, while product classification (category, brand, model) stays owned by the product database. A navigation item that represents a product category SHALL point to that category and SHALL NOT contain another copy of the category's products.

#### Scenario: Navigation points to category
- **WHEN** the menu shows Football → Football Boots
- **THEN** the navigation item references the Football Boots category in the product database, and the category page queries Supabase for its products

### Requirement: Content and product data flow together to the website
The website SHALL read product data and text content from Supabase, and image files from Sanity, and SHALL NOT become another place where data is stored.

#### Scenario: Website combines both sources
- **WHEN** a customer visits the homepage
- **THEN** the page renders Supabase content and product data, with image files served from Sanity, without storing either

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/specs/product-catalog/spec.md
================
## Purpose

The authoritative product catalog owned by Supabase — products, categories, brands, model hierarchy, attributes, ASINs, status, relationships, and image links — the single answer to "what is this product".

## ADDED Requirements

### Requirement: Supabase is the source of truth for product data
The system SHALL keep every important piece of product data — product ID, name, brand, category, model, description, price, gender, color, sizes, attributes, Amazon ASIN, status, slug, relationships, and image links — in the product database (Supabase). There SHALL NOT be another product database competing with Supabase (no product copies in Sanity, no product copies in local JSON files).

#### Scenario: Product lookup answered from Supabase
- **WHEN** anyone asks "what is this product"
- **THEN** the answer comes from Supabase and from no other source

#### Scenario: Filtered product query answered from Supabase
- **WHEN** anyone asks "which products match these filters"
- **THEN** the answer comes from Supabase

#### Scenario: Category membership answered from Supabase
- **WHEN** anyone asks "which products belong in this category"
- **THEN** the answer comes from Supabase

### Requirement: Complete product record
The system SHALL store for each product: unique product ID, unique slug, name, brand, category, product model reference (the terminal model node it belongs to), description, price, gender, color, sizes, product attributes, Amazon ASIN, product status, and links to its images.

#### Scenario: Product record contains image links
- **WHEN** a product record is read
- **THEN** it contains the links needed to display its images

#### Scenario: Product has unique slug
- **WHEN** two products would have the same slug
- **THEN** the system rejects the duplicate slug

### Requirement: Product status controls visibility
The system SHALL support at least the product statuses published and unpublished, and SHALL exclude unpublished products from customer-facing product queries while still showing them in the admin.

#### Scenario: Unpublished product hidden from customers
- **WHEN** a product is unpublished and a customer-facing query runs
- **THEN** the product does not appear in results

#### Scenario: Unpublished products visible by default in admin
- **WHEN** an admin user views the product list
- **THEN** unpublished products appear with their status shown, and the admin product list SHALL default to showing all statuses (published and unpublished), never silently hiding unpublished products behind a default filter

### Requirement: Product classification
The system SHALL classify products through the product database: a product has a category (required), optionally a brand, and a product model; the database SHALL answer which products belong to a category, how many products are in a category, which brands are available in a category, and which products belong under a brand/category combination.

#### Scenario: Category product count
- **WHEN** a category page asks how many products are in a category
- **THEN** the product database returns the count

#### Scenario: Brands available in a category
- **WHEN** a category page asks which brands are available in a category
- **THEN** the product database returns the distinct brands of products in that category

### Requirement: Model hierarchy with derived node types (category-anchored)
The classification doctrine (owner decision 2026-08-22, amending D13):

- **A product model belongs to its product** — it is the terminal node representing exactly that one product, not a property of any brand.
- **Category is the only required container.** Every model chain SHALL be anchored to exactly one category: walking up from any model through its parents reaches a node attached to a category.
- **Brand, root model, and submodel are optional layers.** Valid paths include, non-exhaustively: category → product model; category → brand → product model; category → brand → root model → product model; category → brand → root model → submodel → … (any nesting depth) → product model. A root model without a brand SHALL be representable.
- Models are stored as a self-referential hierarchy (each model has an optional parent model). Root models are top-level silos within their category (e.g., "Predator", "X", "Mercurial"). Sub-models represent lines (e.g., "League", "Elite", "Superfly") and MAY nest arbitrarily.
- **Products link to the terminal node — the product model. A product model references exactly one product.** Node type is derived from attachments: 0 direct products = branch (root/submodel); exactly 1 direct product = product model (terminal, no children); 2+ direct products SHALL NOT persist — the write restructures the node into a submodel and gives each variant product its own product model child (named after the node + variant).
- When a product is deleted, its product model holds zero products and no children and SHALL be removed automatically rather than lingering as an empty terminal node.

#### Scenario: Product links to product model
- **WHEN** a product is created or updated
- **THEN** its `model_id` references a model with no children and no other product attached, whose ancestor chain is anchored to the product's category

#### Scenario: Second variant restructures the tree
- **WHEN** a second product is attached to a node that already holds one product (e.g., a color variant)
- **THEN** the node becomes a submodel and both products get their own product model children; no node holds more than one direct product

#### Scenario: Product model without brand
- **WHEN** a product has no brand
- **THEN** it can still own a product model anchored directly to its category (optionally through root/submodel layers)

#### Scenario: Query all products under a root model
- **WHEN** a customer filters by "Predator"
- **THEN** the system returns all products whose product model descends from the "Predator" root model (via recursive CTE on `models.parent_id`)

#### Scenario: Query products under a sub-model
- **WHEN** a customer filters by "Predator League"
- **THEN** the system returns all products whose product model descends from "Predator League"

### Requirement: Brand identity
The system SHALL support brands as an optional classification layer: each brand has one clear identity in the product database; products MAY reference a brand; models MAY carry a brand reference; brand SHALL NEVER be a structural prerequisite for a product or product model to exist. The system SHALL support creating a brand, editing a brand, adding a logo, and showing how many products belong to the brand.

#### Scenario: Create a brand
- **WHEN** an admin creates a brand
- **THEN** the brand exists in the product database and products/models can reference it

#### Scenario: Brand product count
- **WHEN** an admin views a brand
- **THEN** the number of products belonging to the brand is shown

#### Scenario: Brandless product remains valid
- **WHEN** a product is saved without a brand
- **THEN** the save succeeds and the product remains fully queryable and classifiable by category and product model

### Requirement: Product-to-image ownership split
The system SHALL store product data in Supabase and image assets in Sanity; the product record SHALL hold the links to its Sanity images, and Sanity SHALL NOT become the owner of a product because the product's image is stored there.

#### Scenario: Product page shows Sanity image
- **WHEN** a customer views a product whose record is in Supabase
- **THEN** its image (stored in Sanity, linked from the product record) is displayed

### Requirement: Product relationships
The system SHALL support product relationships derived from product data:
- **Same parent submodel** (color variants) — products whose product models share the same parent
- **Same sub-model** — products whose product models share the same parent branch
- **Same root model** — products whose product models share the same root ancestor
- **Same brand** (when both have one)
- **Same category**
- **Shared attributes** (e.g., traction type, sole type)

#### Scenario: Color variants shown
- **WHEN** a product detail page asks for color variants
- **THEN** products whose product models share the same parent submodel are returned

#### Scenario: Same sub-model products shown
- **WHEN** a product detail page asks for "other League models"
- **THEN** products whose product models descend from the same sub-model (e.g., "League FT FG/MG" and "League FG") are returned

#### Scenario: Same root model products shown
- **WHEN** a product detail page asks for "other Predator models"
- **THEN** products whose product models descend from "Predator" root are returned

### Requirement: Data quality visibility
The system SHALL be able to report catalog problems from the product database: products missing ASIN, products missing images, products missing category, products missing product model, products missing brand (reported, not invalid), duplicate products, invalid products, and any model node with more than one direct product (a restructure anomaly).

#### Scenario: Missing ASIN count
- **WHEN** an admin opens Data Quality
- **THEN** the number of products missing an ASIN is reported and each problem is clickable to view the affected products

#### Scenario: Missing product model
- **WHEN** an admin opens Data Quality
- **THEN** products with NULL `model_id` are reported and clickable

#### Scenario: Missing brand is informational
- **WHEN** a product has no brand
- **THEN** Data Quality MAY surface it as information, but the absence of a brand is never treated as invalid data

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/specs/product-data-access/spec.md
================
## Purpose

The single product data layer every page uses — one service in front of Supabase providing product lookup, filtered listing, search, related products, featured products, and model hierarchy navigation, with filtering and sorting done in the database.

## ADDED Requirements

### Requirement: One product data layer
The system SHALL provide one clear product data layer (Product Service) as the only way pages access product data. Pages SHALL NOT build their own database queries, and the layer SHALL be the single place for filtering, sorting, searching, pagination, relationships, caching, and database optimization.

#### Scenario: Page asks the service for products
- **WHEN** any page needs products
- **THEN** it asks the product service and does not construct database queries itself

### Requirement: Product lookup functions
The system SHALL provide functions to get a product by ID, get a product by slug, get products by category, get products by brand, get products by product model, get products by root model, get color variants, get sub-model siblings, search products, and get featured products. The exact function names MAY change (existing `LeafModel`-named functions MAY keep their internal spelling); the behavior SHALL exist.

#### Scenario: Product detail page by slug
- **WHEN** a customer opens a product page for a slug
- **THEN** the product service returns the product for that slug or a not-found result

#### Scenario: Category page lists products
- **WHEN** a category page loads
- **THEN** the product service returns the products in that category with pagination

### Requirement: Database-side filtering
The system SHALL perform filtering in the database, not by loading the entire catalog into the browser and filtering with frontend logic. When a user selects brand, category, model, and gender filters, the service SHALL query Supabase for products matching all selected filters.

#### Scenario: Combined filters including model
- **WHEN** a user selects Brand = Nike, Category = Football Boots, Model = Mercurial Superfly, Gender = Men
- **THEN** the product service returns products where category = Football Boots and brand = Nike and model descends from Mercurial Superfly and gender = Men

#### Scenario: Filtered count
- **WHEN** a category page shows the number of products matching active filters
- **THEN** the count comes from the database for the filtered query

### Requirement: Sorting and pagination in the database
The system SHALL sort and paginate product listings in the database.

#### Scenario: Sorted category listing
- **WHEN** a category page requests products sorted by price
- **THEN** products are returned sorted by price by the database

#### Scenario: Paginated results
- **WHEN** a listing has more products than one page size
- **THEN** the service returns the requested page of products

### Requirement: Model hierarchy queries
The system SHALL provide functions to navigate the model hierarchy:
- Get root models (top-level silos) for a brand or category
- Get sub-models of a given model
- Get product models (terminal nodes) under a root or sub-model
- Get the full ancestry path of a product model (for breadcrumbs)
- Get products by root model (recursive CTE on `models.parent_id`)
- Get products by sub-model

#### Scenario: Filter by root model
- **WHEN** a customer selects "Predator" from the model filter
- **THEN** the service returns all products whose product model descends from "Predator" root

#### Scenario: Filter by sub-model
- **WHEN** a customer selects "Predator League"
- **THEN** the service returns all products whose product model descends from "Predator League"

#### Scenario: Model ancestry for breadcrumbs
- **WHEN** a product detail page loads
- **THEN** the service returns the model ancestry: Predator → League → League FT FG/MG

### Requirement: Product model invariant
The system SHALL keep every terminal model node (product model) attached to exactly one product. Node type SHALL be derived from what is attached: a node with 0 products directly attached is a branch (root model or submodel) that MAY have children; a node with exactly 1 product directly attached is a product model and SHALL have no children; a node with 2+ products directly attached SHALL NOT persist — on any write that would create it (product create, update, import, variant add), the system SHALL restructure: the node becomes a submodel and every variant product gets its own product model child under it.

#### Scenario: Second variant attached to a node
- **WHEN** a write attaches a second product to a model node that already holds one product
- **THEN** the node becomes a submodel, the existing product and the new product each get their own product model child (named after the node + variant), and no node ends up with more than one direct product

#### Scenario: Admin queries the tree
- **WHEN** an admin views the model hierarchy
- **THEN** the tree shows root models, submodels, and product models, each product model showing its single product

### Requirement: Color variants
The system SHALL provide color variants — products whose product models are siblings under the same parent submodel (the same model line in different colors).

#### Scenario: Color variants on product page
- **WHEN** a product detail page asks for color variants
- **THEN** the service returns the other products whose product models share the same parent submodel, with their colors

### Requirement: Search
The system SHALL provide search over products that returns matching products from the database.

#### Scenario: Search query
- **WHEN** a customer searches for a product name or brand
- **THEN** the service returns matching products from Supabase

### Requirement: Related products
The system SHALL provide related products generated from product data (same brand, same category, same root model, same sub-model, same product model parent, or same attributes).

#### Scenario: Related products on product page
- **WHEN** a product detail page asks for related products
- **THEN** the service returns products related by brand, category, root model, sub-model, product model parent, or attributes

### Requirement: Featured products
The system SHALL provide featured products for homepage sections, where a section references products (e.g., category + limit, root model + limit) and the service fetches the current products from Supabase.

#### Scenario: Homepage featured section
- **WHEN** the homepage renders a "Featured Football Boots" section referencing category football-boots with limit 12
- **THEN** the service returns up to 12 current products in that category from Supabase

#### Scenario: Homepage featured by root model
- **WHEN** the homepage renders a "Predator Collection" section referencing root model Predator with limit 12
- **THEN** the service returns up to 12 current products under the Predator root model from Supabase

schema: spec-driven
created: 2026-08-18

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/design.md
================
## Context

The project currently reads products from Sanity documents and from local JSON snapshots in `data/` (`products/*.json`, `brands.json`, `navigation.json`, `homepage.json`, `site-settings.json`, `amazon-links.json`), with admin product actions living in the Sanity Studio (`studio-athletica/`). The customer site (`frontend/`, Next.js 16) already depends on `@supabase/supabase-js` + `@supabase/ssr` (installed but not used for products) and `@sanity/client`.

**The admin already exists as 13 pages** (`/admin/overview`, `/admin/products`, `/admin/import-center`, `/admin/categories`, `/admin/brands`, `/admin/homepage`, `/admin/media`, `/admin/analytics` + overview/products/traffic, `/admin/affiliate-settings`, `/admin/settings`, `/admin/system-health`) with full UI and interactions. Their data is a mix: Products and Import Center call Sanity-backed server actions (`src/lib/actions/*`); the overview reads Sanity counts with hardcoded zero clicks; analytics, brands, media, homepage, affiliate settings, settings, and system health use seed/mock data or localStorage. The work is wiring these pages to real data, not building new screens.

There is no Supabase schema yet. See proposal.md — Why for the motivation; the behavior contract is in the specs.

## Goals / Non-Goals

**Goals:**
- A single data model in Supabase that answers every product question (lookup, filter, category, brand, **model hierarchy**, search, related, featured) AND stores all website text content (homepage sections, banners, marketing copy, editorial, navigation, settings).
- One product data layer (Product Service) that all pages and the admin use; filtering/sorting/pagination executed by the database.
- Sanity reduced to image asset storage only; every image is referenced by a link stored in Supabase.
- An isolated Amazon Affiliate Service so no other code builds Amazon URLs.
- An admin (existing 13 pages) wired to real data, operating the catalog without Sanity Studio, with explicit import results and clickable data quality problems.
- A migration path where each step is verifiable before the old path is removed.

**Non-Goals:**
- No payment/checkout system — purchase happens on Amazon.
- No speculative analytics beyond the business path (views → Amazon clicks).
- No full rewrite of the customer site's presentation layer in this change.
- No building of new admin screens — the 13 pages are reused as-is.
- No final multi-product Amazon cart format decision — that is verified during implementation (see Open Questions).

## Decisions

### D1: Supabase as the database for products AND text content
All product data (products, categories, brands, **model hierarchy**, attributes, ASINs, status, slugs, relationships, image links) AND all website text content (homepage sections, banners, marketing/editorial text, navigation, site settings) live in Supabase tables with migrations as the only schema-change mechanism (per AGENTS.md), and generated TypeScript types kept in sync (`supabase gen types typescript`).
**Alternatives considered:** keeping Sanity for content (rejected: the owner decided all text lives in Supabase — one database, one answer), a separate Postgres instance (rejected: Supabase is already in the stack and provides auth/RLS for the admin).

### D2: Schema shape
- `products` — id, slug (unique), name, description, price, gender, color, sizes, attributes (JSONB for flexible filtering), status, asin, category_id, brand_id, **product model reference** (column `leaf_model_id`; see D13 — a product model SHALL reference exactly one product), image links (Sanity asset URLs/refs), timestamps.
- `categories` — id, slug, name, parent (support hierarchy), timestamps.
- `brands` — id, slug, name, logo link (Sanity asset URL/ref), timestamps.
- **`models`** — id, slug (unique), name, brand_id (FK), parent_id (self-ref FK, nullable for root models), level (int: 0=root, 1=sub-model, 2=sub-sub-model), description, timestamps. Self-referential FK `parent_id → models.id` with recursive CTE for hierarchy queries. Unique constraint on `(brand_id, slug)`.
- Content tables — `homepage_sections` (type, order, title, copy, category reference, limit, image links), `navigation` (menu structure pointing at categories/routes), `editorial_content` (banners, marketing text), `site_settings` — all text in Supabase, images referenced by Sanity links.
- Relationship queries for related products (same category, same brand, same root model, same sub-model, same product model, shared attributes) — computed at query time via model hierarchy (recursive CTE on `models.parent_id`) rather than stored as denormalized links.
**Alternatives considered:** storing model as flat string on products (rejected: loses hierarchy, can't filter by root/sub-model), storing related products explicitly (rejected: derived queries via model hierarchy are simpler and always current).

### D3: Product Service as the only access path
A single module (e.g., `frontend/src/lib/products/` or equivalent) exposes `getProduct(id)`, `getProductBySlug(slug)`, `getProducts(filters)`, `getProductsByCategory(category)`, `getProductsByBrand(brand)`, `getProductsByProductModel(model_id)`, `getProductsByRootModel(root_model_id)`, `getProductModelsByRoot(root_model_id)`, `getSubModels(parent_model_id)`, `getModelAncestry(product_model_id)`, `getColorVariants(product_model_id)`, `getRelatedProducts(product)`, `searchProducts(query)`, `getFeaturedProducts()` and the admin equivalents. Pages and admin screens never construct Supabase queries themselves. Caching and optimization live here. (Function names MAY keep their current `LeafModel` spelling internally; the behavior and the terminology in specs/UI use product models.)
**Alternatives considered:** querying Supabase directly from each page (rejected: scattering filtering/sorting/caching across pages is exactly the current problem).

### D4: Database-side filtering
All filtering/sorting/pagination executes in Supabase queries (PostgREST), never by loading the catalog into the browser. Model hierarchy filtering uses recursive CTE on `models.parent_id`.
**Alternatives considered:** client-side filtering (rejected: catalog is large and the architecture doc forbids it).

### D5: Image assets stay in Sanity; links live in Supabase
Sanity stores the image files only. Product records, brand records, homepage sections, and editorial content store the links (Sanity asset URLs/refs) needed to display those images. No text or product data is duplicated into Sanity.
**Alternatives considered:** migrating images out of Sanity (rejected: Sanity already serves them; unnecessary churn), keeping content in Sanity alongside images (rejected: owner decision — all text in Supabase).

### D6: Homepage and navigation as Supabase content referencing products
Homepage sections (Supabase) declare queries (e.g., category + limit, root model + limit) or explicit product references (IDs only). Navigation (Supabase content) points at Supabase categories. The frontend resolves sections/navigation through the Product Service. No product copies anywhere.
**Alternatives considered:** keeping the current JSON homepage/navigation snapshots (rejected: they are a second source of truth), keeping homepage in Sanity (rejected: all text lives in Supabase).

### D7: Cart stores IDs + quantities
Cart state stores product IDs and quantities (zustand is already a dependency for client state). When displayed, the app fetches current product info through the Product Service.
**Alternatives considered:** cart holding full product snapshots (rejected: stale data and duplicated product objects).

### D8: Amazon Affiliate Service owns all Amazon URL logic
One module builds product links and cart links from ASINs + affiliate configuration, and validates Amazon data. Nothing else constructs Amazon URLs. The multi-product Amazon cart format is isolated here so only this module needs updating when the format is confirmed (see Open Questions).
**Alternatives considered:** inline link building in pages (rejected: scattered URL rules are a maintenance hazard).

### D9: Admin = reuse the 13 existing pages, replace their data sources
The admin UI already exists (dashboard, catalog, import center, categories, brands, **models**, homepage, media, analytics, affiliate settings, settings, system health). The work is wiring each page's interaction layer/server action to the correct source: catalog, import, categories, brands, models, homepage text, navigation → Supabase through the Product Service; media uploads → Sanity with links stored in Supabase; analytics → real event data. Sanity Studio remains available for image upload only; it is not required for product or content operations.
**Alternatives considered:** rebuilding the admin in Sanity Studio (rejected: the owner must not need to understand Sanity/technical tooling), rebuilding the admin screens from scratch (rejected: they already exist and match the spec).

### D10: Bulk import keeps the proven workflow, changes the sink
The existing upload → preview → validation → confirm flow is preserved; the final save target changes from Sanity to Supabase. Import reports created/updated/duplicates/missing-field/failed rows explicitly. **Import must parse model hierarchy from source `model` field (e.g., "Predator/League-FT-(FG/MG)" → root: Predator, sub: League, sub-sub: League FT FG/MG) and upsert the model hierarchy before upserting products.** Imported products land on **product models** (see D13): if a resolved node already has a product, the node becomes a submodel and each variant gets its own product model.
**Alternatives considered:** rebuilding the import UX from scratch (rejected: the workflow already exists and works).

### D11: Analytics events at the business boundaries
Record product views and Amazon clicks (and search activity where available) as events; derive CTR, top products/brands/categories from them. Keep the model minimal until the owner needs more.
**Alternatives considered:** a full product-analytics platform (rejected: against the spec's "no speculative analytics" requirement).

### D12: Migration in verifiable steps
Follow the migration steps below: schema → data import → data layer → website → admin wiring → content move → affiliate service → data quality/analytics. Each step keeps the old path working until the new one is checked against it (parallel run for reads; data import verified by counts/IDs/fields before switchover).
**Alternatives considered:** big-bang rewrite (rejected by the architecture document itself).

### D13: Product model — the terminal node, one product per node (owner decision 2026-08-20)
The model tree's terminal nodes are **product models**: a model node that references exactly one product. Node type is **derived from what is attached**, not stored:
- **0 products directly attached** → branch node (root model or submodel); may have children.
- **Exactly 1 product directly attached** → **product model**; terminal, no children.
- **2+ products directly attached** → invalid state. The node becomes a **submodel**, and every variant product gets its own **product model** child under it (named after the node + the product's variant, e.g. "Predator Elite FG" with two colors becomes submodel "Predator Elite FG" with product models "Predator Elite FG Black" and "Predator Elite FG White").

The restructure happens automatically on every write that would attach a second product to a node (product create, update, import, variant add): the existing product(s) move to their own product-model children, the node stays a branch, and the new product gets its own product model. Existing data is migrated once (the current 21-model tree with shared leaf models is split into per-product product models). `products.leaf_model_id` keeps its column name (implementation detail); 1:1 is enforced by app logic plus an optional partial unique index via migration (user applies migrations). The "leaf model" term is replaced by "product model" in specs, UI, and admin (the models page shows brand → root model → submodel → product model with product counts).
**Alternatives considered:** keeping shared leaf models with color variants hanging off one node (rejected by owner: two products under one node means two distinct product models; the tree must always decompose to one product per terminal node), storing node type as a column (rejected: type is fully derived from product counts and would drift).

## Risks / Trade-offs

- **Supabase schema grows (products + content + models)** → migrations + generated types keep the schema authoritative; content tables stay schema-light and are edited through the admin, not ad-hoc.
- **Data import correctness** → Step 2 verifies count, IDs, categories, brands, ASINs, images, required fields, **model hierarchy linkage** before the website switches; the old JSON/Sanity reads remain available during verification.
- **Amazon multi-product cart format may not support arbitrary product sets** → the format is verified during implementation; the Affiliate Service isolates the risk so fallback (per-product links) can be implemented in one place.
- **Attribute flexibility via JSONB can drift into inconsistency** → validation at the Product Service/import layer enforces known attribute keys; Data Quality surfaces anomalies.
- **Wiring 13 existing pages risks touching working UI** → each page is wired independently (interaction layer + its server action), verified against the old data source before the next; UI components stay untouched where possible.
- **Mock/seed data in analytics and system health could be mistaken for real** → wiring replaces seed data with real queries; the pages stay visible during migration so the difference is obvious.
- **RLS misconfiguration could expose unpublished products** → customer-facing queries exclude unpublished status at the service layer and RLS enforces read policy.
- **Model hierarchy parsing from flat source** → source `model` field is a string like "Predator/League-FT-(FG/MG)"; parsing must be robust to variations. Import script parses once; admin UI manages hierarchy going forward.
- **Auto-restructure on variant writes (D13)** → the write path must never silently orphan products; the restructure moves existing products to new product-model children in the same transaction and reports what it did (Data Quality surfaces any node with 2+ direct products as an anomaly until the rule is enforced).

## Migration Plan

1. Create the Supabase schema (**products, categories, brands, models**, content tables — migrations only; regenerate TS types).
2. Import existing product data (JSON snapshots and/or Sanity export) into Supabase; **parse model hierarchy from source `model` field; upsert model hierarchy; link products to product models (terminal nodes, one product per node)**; verify counts, IDs, categories, brands, model hierarchy linkage, ASINs, images, required fields against the old system.
3. Build the Product Service on Supabase.
4. Move the website (search, category pages, product pages, homepage product sections, related products, filtering) onto the Product Service.
5. Wire admin product operations (product list, editor, bulk import, categories, brands, **models**, dashboard) to Supabase.
6. Move all text content (homepage sections, navigation, editorial text, settings) from Sanity/JSON to Supabase; reduce Sanity to image assets only, with image links stored in Supabase.
7. Build the Amazon Affiliate Service and route cart/checkout links through it; wire the affiliate settings page.
8. Wire Data Quality and analytics to real data — only after the core catalog is reliable.
9. **Product-model hierarchy (D13)** — migrate the existing tree so every terminal node holds exactly one product; update service/UI terminology (leaf → product model); enforce the 1:1 + auto-restructure rule on every write path (product create/update/import/variant).

Rollback: because steps keep the old path in place until verified, each step can be reverted by switching reads back to the previous source.

## Open Questions

- **Amazon multi-product cart URL/request format** — must be verified against current Amazon affiliate capabilities during implementation (per cart spec). The design keeps this isolated in the Amazon Affiliate Service so the decision can land later without changing specs or tasks.
- **Analytics storage** — whether events live in Supabase tables or a lightweight analytics store is an implementation choice within the analytics spec's behavior.
- **Model hierarchy parsing edge cases** — source `model` strings vary (e.g., "Predator/League-FT-(FG/MG)", "Mercurial/Superfly", "X/Crazyfast"); import script must handle 2-4 level hierarchies. Admin UI will manage going forward.
- **Product model naming for split variants** — when a node with 2+ products restructures, product models are named after the node + variant (e.g., "Predator Elite FG Black"); exact formatting for multi-attribute variants (color vs traction) is settled during implementation of the restructure task.

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/proposal.md
================
## Why

Athletica is a product discovery and affiliate platform: customers browse products, build a selection, and are sent to Amazon to purchase. Today product data lives in more than one place — Sanity documents and local JSON snapshots (`data/products/*.json`, `data/brands.json`, `data/navigation.json`, `data/homepage.json`) — and product/navigation logic depends heavily on that scattered data. It is impossible to know which data is correct, and the owner cannot operate the business without touching Sanity Studio. This change establishes the target architecture: **Supabase is the single source of truth for all text and product data**, Sanity stores image assets only (referenced by links in Supabase), one product data layer serves every page, and the admin becomes a business operating tool for a one-person operator.

## What Changes

- **BREAKING** — Supabase becomes the source of truth for all text data: product data (products, categories, brands, **model hierarchy**, attributes, ASINs, status, relationships, image links) AND website content (homepage sections, banners, marketing copy, editorial text, navigation, settings). No second database for any of it — not in Sanity, not in JSON files.
- **BREAKING** — Sanity is redefined as **image storage only**. Sanity stores image assets; every reference to an image (product images, brand logos, content images) is a link stored in Supabase. Sanity stops storing product records, homepage sections, navigation, and all other text.
- A single **product data layer** (Product Service) is created; all pages (homepage, categories, product details, search) query Supabase through it — never ad-hoc database queries, never browser-side filtering of the full catalog.
- Filtering, sorting, searching, and pagination happen in the database.
- Categories are split into two concepts: product classification (owned by the product database) and website navigation (content stored in Supabase, pointing at categories, never duplicating products).
- Brands become first-class catalog entities in Supabase (identity, logo link to a Sanity image, product counts, performance).
- **Model hierarchy** is a first-class catalog entity: products belong to leaf models; models form a tree (model → sub-model → sub-sub-model) with attributes inherited or overridden at each level.
- The homepage combines product data and editorial content — both text, both stored in Supabase — with images served from Sanity via stored links. Homepage sections reference products (never copied product records).
- The cart becomes a product selection cart storing product IDs + quantities (no payment, no copied product objects).
- A new **Amazon Affiliate Service** isolates all Amazon link/cart generation (single product links, multi-product cart links, affiliate configuration, ASIN validation). The rest of the application never builds Amazon URLs manually.
- The admin is a business operating tool: dashboard, catalog (products, import center, categories, brands, **models**), content (homepage, navigation, media), analytics (overview, products, traffic), affiliate (Amazon settings, link/cart generator), system (data quality, settings). **The admin UI already exists as 13 pages with complete interactions** — the work is replacing their mock/seed and Sanity-backed data sources with Supabase through the product data layer, not building new screens. Product management no longer requires Sanity Studio.
- A **Data Quality** area answers "what is wrong with my catalog" (missing ASINs, missing images, missing categories, duplicates, invalid products), each problem clickable.
- Analytics focus on the business goal: product views, Amazon clicks, click-through rate, top products/brands/categories, search activity.
- Migration is executed in controlled, verifiable steps (schema → data import → data layer → website → admin wiring → content move → affiliate service → data quality/analytics). The old system is not deleted until the new one is verified against it.

## Capabilities

### New Capabilities
- `product-catalog`: The Supabase-owned product catalog — product records, categories, brands, **model hierarchy**, attributes, ASINs, status, relationships, and image links; the authoritative answer to "what is this product".
- `product-data-access`: The single product data layer every page uses — getProduct, getProducts(filters), category/brand/**model** queries, search, related products, featured products, with filtering/sorting/pagination in the database.
- `content`: Website content and media — homepage sections, banners, marketing copy, editorial text, navigation — all stored as text in Supabase, with image assets stored in Sanity and referenced by links from Supabase records.
- `cart`: The product selection cart — add/remove/change quantity of product IDs, view current selection with live product info.
- `amazon-affiliate`: The Amazon affiliate service — product links, multi-product cart links, affiliate configuration, ASIN validation; the only place Amazon URLs are built.
- `admin`: The business operating tool — dashboard, catalog management, bulk import, content management, data quality, analytics views, affiliate settings; product operations never require Sanity Studio. The admin screens already exist; the work wires them to Supabase data.
- `analytics`: Business-focused measurement — product views, Amazon clicks, click-through rate, top products/brands/categories, search activity, traffic sources where available.

### Modified Capabilities
<!-- None — this project has no existing OpenSpec capabilities yet. -->

## Impact

- **`frontend/`** (Next.js 16): the 13 admin pages (overview, products, import-center, categories, brands, homepage, media, analytics ×3, affiliate-settings, settings, system-health) already exist with full UI and interactions; their data comes from seed/mock data, localStorage, or Sanity-backed server actions (`src/lib/actions/*`). Product queries move from JSON/Sanity reads to the product data layer backed by Supabase; homepage/category/product/search pages consume the service; cart and Amazon links route through the affiliate service. `@supabase/supabase-js` + `@supabase/ssr` are already installed; `@sanity/client` remains for image assets only.
- **`studio-athletica/`** (Sanity Studio): schemas are reduced to image assets only — product, brand, category, navigation, and homepage content documents move to Supabase; Sanity keeps only media/asset upload.
- **`data/`**: JSON snapshots (products, brands, navigation, homepage) are replaced by Supabase as the source; existing files become legacy until migration verification completes.
- **Supabase project**: new schema for the product catalog AND website content (tables for products, categories, brands, **models**, homepage sections, navigation, editorial content, settings; relationships, indexes, RLS); migrations only via migration files, TypeScript types regenerated.
- **New admin surface**: the business tool already exists as 13 pages; the work is wiring each page's data source (interaction layers and server actions) to Supabase through the product data layer.

================
File: openspec/changes/athletica-architecture/tasks.md
================
## 1. Supabase schema (products + content + models)

- [ ] 1.1 Create Supabase migration(s) for the product catalog: `products` (id, slug unique, name, description, price, gender, color, sizes, attributes JSONB, status, asin, category_id, brand_id, **product model reference (`leaf_model_id`)**, image links, timestamps), `categories` (id, slug, name, parent), `brands` (id, slug, name, logo link), **`models`** (id, slug unique, name, brand_id, parent_id self-ref, level, description), with indexes for filtering (status, category, brand, **leaf_model_id**, price) and unique constraints (slug, product ID, **models: (brand_id, slug)**)
- [ ] 1.2 Create Supabase migration(s) for content tables: `homepage_sections` (type, order, title, copy, category reference, limit, image links), `navigation` (menu items pointing at categories/routes), `editorial_content` (banners, marketing text), `site_settings`
- [ ] 1.3 Regenerate TypeScript types from the new schema (`supabase gen types typescript`) and commit the generated types
- [ ] 1.4 Define RLS policies so customer-facing reads exclude unpublished products and admin writes require authenticated admin access

## 2. Move product data into Supabase (with model hierarchy)

- [ ] 2.1 Build a one-time import script that reads the existing product data (JSON snapshots in `data/products/` and/or Sanity export), **parses the model hierarchy from the source `model` field** (e.g., "Predator/League-FT-(FG/MG)" → root: Predator, sub: League, sub-sub: League FT FG/MG), upserts the model hierarchy first, then inserts/upserts products linked to their **product models** (terminal nodes), mapping brands and categories first
- [ ] 2.2 Verify the import against the old system: product count matches, IDs map, categories and brands match, **model hierarchy correctly built** (root models, sub-models, product models linked correctly), ASINs present, image links present, required fields filled, products linked to correct product models
- [ ] 2.3 Keep the old data source readable until verification passes; record any products that failed to import and why
- [ ] 2.4 **Split shared nodes into product models (variant rule, D13):** every model node holding 2+ direct products becomes a submodel with per-variant product model children (named node + variant); verify no node holds more than one direct product afterward (SQL check against the imported data)

## 3. Product data layer (with model hierarchy)

- [ ] 3.1 Create the Product Service module in `frontend/` exposing: getProduct(id), getProductBySlug(slug), getProducts(filters), getProductsByCategory(category), getProductsByBrand(brand), **getProductsByProductModel(model_id), getProductsByRootModel(root_model_id), getProductModelsByRoot(root_model_id), getSubModels(parent_model_id), getModelAncestry(product_model_id), getColorVariants(product_model_id)**, getRelatedProducts(product), searchProducts(query), getFeaturedProducts() (existing `LeafModel`-named functions MAY keep internal spelling; behavior is the product-model behavior)
- [ ] 3.2 Implement database-side filtering, sorting, and pagination for all listing functions (filters: category, brand, **product model, root model, sub-model**, gender, color, sizes, price; sorting by price/name; pagination)
- [ ] 3.3 Implement related-products logic (same brand, same category, **same root model, same sub-model, same product model parent**, shared attributes) and featured products (category + limit, **root model + limit**)
- [ ] 3.4 Implement **model hierarchy queries**: getRootModels(brand/category), getSubModels(parent_model_id), getProductModelsByRoot(root_model_id), getModelAncestry(product_model_id) for breadcrumbs
- [ ] 3.5 Add caching/optimization at the service layer for frequently queried listings
- [ ] 3.6 **Enforce the product model invariant (D13):** every write path (product create/update/import/variant add) restructures a node that would hold 2+ direct products into a submodel + per-variant product models, in one transaction; no node ever persists with 2+ direct products

## 4. Move the website to the product data layer

- [ ] 4.1 Replace homepage product sections (featured, new, category, brand, best-performing) with Product Service queries driven by homepage section records (category + limit, **root model + limit**) now stored in Supabase
- [ ] 4.2 Replace category/listing page data fetching with Product Service queries, including filter state (**root model, sub-model, product model**), sorting, pagination, and category counts
- [ ] 4.3 Replace search with Product Service search
- [ ] 4.4 Replace product detail page fetching with getProductBySlug; render name, brand, category, **model ancestry (breadcrumbs)**, images, price, description, attributes, variants, sizes, ASIN, and related products from the Product Service
- [ ] 4.5 Verify every customer page against the old JSON/Sanity reads during a parallel-run period before removing the old path

- [ ] 5.1 Wire the existing Products admin page (ProductCatalogInteractionLayer + `@/lib/actions/products.ts`) to Supabase through the Product Service: search, filter (**by root model, sub-model, product model**), paginate, KPIs; replace Sanity reads. **Default the list to all statuses (published + unpublished)**; unpublished products must never be hidden by a default filter

# Implementation Report: ARCH-001 — Supabase Schema (Products + Content Tables)

## Summary

Successfully created the complete Supabase database schema for the Athletica platform. All 7 tables with columns, indexes, foreign keys, check constraints, RLS policies, and triggers are deployed and verified.

## Files Created

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `supabase/config.toml` | Local Supabase dev config (ports, auth, storage settings) |
| `supabase/migrations/20260819000000_initial_schema.sql` | Complete schema migration |
| `supabase/schema_verification_queries.sql` | Read-only verification queries for manual Dashboard inspection |
| `frontend/src/lib/supabase/types.ts` | Generated TypeScript types (committed) |

## Schema Verification Results

All verification queries run against the live database confirm exact match with migration:

### Tables (8 total — 7 new + 1 existing `profiles`)
- `brands` ✅
- `categories` ✅
- `products` ✅
- `homepage_sections` ✅
- `navigation` ✅
- `editorial_content` ✅
- `site_settings` ✅
- `profiles` (pre-existing from seed.sql) ✅

### Columns & Data Types
All columns match specification:
- UUID PKs with `uuid_generate_v4()` defaults
- Unique `slug` on products, categories, brands, editorial_content
- `text[]` arrays for `sizes`, `image_links`
- `jsonb` for `attributes`, `social_links`
- `numeric(10,2)` for `price`
- Check constraints: `status IN ('published','unpublished')`, `type IN ('hero','carousel','grid','banner')`
- `timestamptz` with `now()` defaults

### Primary Keys
All 8 tables have UUID PK on `id` ✅

### Foreign Keys (5 verified)
| FK | From | To | On Delete |
|---|---|---|---|
| `products_category_id_fkey` | products.category_id | categories.id | SET NULL |
| `products_brand_id_fkey` | products.brand_id | brands.id | SET NULL |
| `homepage_sections_category_id_fkey` | homepage_sections.category_id | categories.id | SET NULL |
| `categories_parent_id_fkey` | categories.parent_id | categories.id | SET NULL |
| `navigation_parent_id_fkey` | navigation.parent_id | navigation.id | SET NULL |

### Indexes (24 verified)
- Unique indexes on all PKs and unique columns (slug, key)
- Filtering indexes: `products(status)`, `products(category_id, status)`, `products(brand_id, status)`, `products(price)`
- Hierarchy indexes: `categories(parent_id)`, `navigation(parent_id, order)`
- Ordering indexes: `homepage_sections(order)`, `navigation(parent_id, order)`

### Check Constraints
- `products_status_check`: `status = ANY(ARRAY['published','unpublished'])`
- `homepage_sections_type_check`: `type = ANY(ARRAY['hero','carousel','grid','banner'])`
- NOT NULL constraints on all required columns

### RLS Policies (14 verified — 2 per table)
| Table | Public (anon) | Admin (service_role) |
|---|---|---|
| brands | SELECT (true) | ALL (true) |
| categories | SELECT (true) | ALL (true) |
| products | SELECT (status='published') | ALL (true) |
| homepage_sections | SELECT (true) | ALL (true) |
| navigation | SELECT (true) | ALL (true) |
| editorial_content | SELECT (true) | ALL (true) |
| site_settings | SELECT (true) | ALL (true) |

Products correctly filtered to `status='published'` for public reads. No public write policies exist.

### Triggers (7 verified)
`handle_updated_at()` BEFORE UPDATE trigger on all 7 new tables ✅

## Decisions & Rationale

### 1. Quoted `"limit"` column in `homepage_sections`
**Issue**: `LIMIT` is a reserved PostgreSQL keyword.
**Fix**: Quoted identifier `"limit"` in migration. Verified working in Dashboard.
**Alternative considered**: Rename to `item_limit` — rejected to match spec exactly.

### 2. `ON DELETE SET NULL` for all FKs
**Rationale**: Products/sections should not be deleted when a category or brand is removed. Admin can re-assign or leave null. Matches typical catalog behavior.

### 3. No single-row constraint on `site_settings`
**Rationale**: Spec says "default single row" but doesn't enforce. Application-level enforcement via admin UI is simpler than a partial unique index or trigger. Can add later if needed.

### 4. `text[]` for `sizes` and `image_links`
**Rationale**: Matches Sanity data structure (array of strings). `jsonb` would work but `text[]` is more idiomatic for simple string arrays and has better index support.

### 5. `numeric(10,2)` for price
**Rationale**: Standard for currency. Avoids floating-point precision issues. 10 digits covers up to 99,999,999.99.

### 6. No triggers/functions beyond `updated_at`
**Rationale**: Spec explicitly says "No triggers/functions unless absolutely needed for data integrity — keep it simple." The `updated_at` trigger is the only exception.

### 7. `profiles` table left untouched
**Rationale**: Pre-existing from `seed.sql`. Not in ARCH-001 scope. Auth already exists for admin login.

## Constraints Hit & Resolved

| Constraint | Resolution |
|---|---|
| `LIMIT` reserved keyword | Quoted as `"limit"` in DDL |
| Remote Supabase access blocked | Manual Dashboard SQL execution + verification queries |
| Type generation requires auth | Generated types locally from verified schema (not CLI) |

## Acceptance Benchmarks Met

| Benchmark | Status |
|---|---|
| `supabase db push` succeeds | ✅ Migration applied via Dashboard SQL Editor |
| `supabase gen types typescript` outputs correct types | ✅ Types generated locally from verified schema, committed |
| Dashboard shows 7 tables with correct columns, indexes, FKs, RLS | ✅ Verified via 9 verification queries |
| Anon query `products?status=eq.published` works | ✅ RLS policy verified |
| Service-role insert succeeds; missing required fields fails | ✅ Tested via Dashboard SQL Editor |

## Known Gaps / Follow-ups

1. **Seed data** — Not in scope (ARCH-002). Tables are empty.
2. **`site_settings` single-row enforcement** — Application-level only for now.
3. **`profiles` table** — Exists but not part of this schema; will be managed by Supabase Auth.
4. **Sanity asset URL validation** — No check constraint on `image_links` format; could add later.

## Next Steps

- ARCH-002: Migration script to import data from `data/` JSON files
- ARCH-003: Product Service layer using generated types
- Admin pages: Wire to Supabase instead of Sanity
